- **Why Long Games (117.6 Avg Turns)?**
  Passive play (e.g., ending turns early) allows the opponent to dominate resource acquisition and board control, prolonging the game until the opponent wins.

| **API**               | **Purpose**                                                                 | **Why It Matters**                                                                 |
|-----------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| `copy_game`           | Simulate future states after taking an action.                              | Enables lookahead to evaluate action outcomes.                                   |
| `make_value_fn`      | Build a heuristic to score game states (e.g., VP, resource production).   | Critical for prioritizing actions that maximize VP or board control.            |
| `chance_children`     | Expand stochastic outcomes (dice, dev cards, robber).                       | Handles uncertainty; evaluates expected value of actions under chance.          |
| `pruned_actions`      | Filter legal actions to reduce search space.                                | Improves efficiency by focusing on high-impact actions (e.g., builds, trades). |

#### **4. Prioritized Strategic Flaws**
| **Flaw**                          | **Impact**               | **Fix**                                                                                     | **Complexity** | **Expected VP Impact** |
|------------------------------------|--------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|----------------|------------------------|
| No action evaluation               | Large                    | Implement 1-ply lookahead: score actions using `copy_game` + `make_value_fn`.               | Low            | Large                  |
| No chance handling                 | Medium                   | Use `chance_children` to evaluate expected value under dice/dev/robber outcomes.             | Medium         | Medium                 |
| No end-turn policy                 | Medium                   | Add logic to avoid ending turns prematurely (e.g., build if resources allow).               | Low            | Medium                 |
| No robber/knight policy            | Small                    | Prioritize `MOVE_ROBBER` to steal resources or block opponents.                              | Medium         | Small                  |

---

#### **5. Recommended Minimal-Change Intervention**
**Algorithm**: 1-ply lookahead with `make_value_fn("base_fn")`.
**Steps**:
1. For each `action` in `playable_actions`:
   - Use `copy_game(game)` to simulate the action.
   - Apply `execute(copied_game, action)`.
   - Score the resulting state with `value_fn = make_value_fn("base_fn")`.
2. Select the action with the **highest score**.
3. **Tie-breaker**: Prefer actions that build settlements/cities/roads.

**Example Code**:
```python
def decide(self, game, playable_actions):
    value_fn = make_value_fn("base_fn")
    best_action = None
    best_score = -float('inf')

    for action in playable_actions:
        copied_game = copy_game(game)
        execute(copied_game, action, validate=False)
        score = value_fn(copied_game, self.color)
        if score > best_score:
            best_score = score
            best_action = action
    return best_action
```

   - **Pass**: Avg VP > 4.0 (indicates progression beyond initial settlements).
   - **Fail**: Avg VP ≤ 2.0 (no improvement).
2. **Test**: Check if the player builds ≥1 city/road in 50% of games.
   - **Pass**: Avg cities/roads > 0.5.

3. **Test**: Verify no "Default Action" logs in `game_output.txt`.
   - **Pass**: Logs show "Choosing action with score: X".
   - **Fail**: "Choosing First Action" persists.

---

#### **7. Pitfalls to Avoid**
- **Timeouts**: Limit simulation depth (1-ply only).
- **Shared State Mutation**: Always use `copy_game` before simulating actions.
- **Invalid Actions**: Use `validate=False` in `execute` for speed, but ensure actions are legal.
- **Overfitting**: Use `base_fn` for generality; avoid custom heuristics initially.

### **1. Algorithm for `decide()` Method**

     - Score the resulting state: `score = value_fn(copied, self.color)`.
   - Track the action with the highest score.

4. **Tie-Breaking**:
   - If scores are tied, prefer actions in the order listed above.

5. **Fallback**:
   - If no build/trade/play actions exist, return `END_TURN`.

### **6. Example Code Snippet for `decide()`**
```python
def decide(self, game, playable_actions):
    # Filter END_TURN if builds/trades exist
    filtered_actions = [
        a for a in playable_actions
        if a.action_type != ActionType.END_TURN or
           not any(a.action_type in {ActionType.BUILD_CITY, ActionType.BUILD_SETTLEMENT, ActionType.BUILD_ROAD} for a in playable_actions)
    ]

    # Cap actions to evaluate
    max_actions = 20
    if len(filtered_actions) > max_actions:
        filtered_actions = sorted(
            filtered_actions,
            key=lambda a: (a.action_type != ActionType.END_TURN, a.action_type.value),
            reverse=True
        )[:max_actions]

    # Simulate and score
    value_fn = make_value_fn("base_fn", DEFAULT_WEIGHTS)
    best_action, best_score = None, -float('inf')
    for action in filtered_actions:
        copied = copy_game(game)
        execute(copied, action, validate=False)
        score = value_fn(copied, self.color)
        if score > best_score:
            best_action, best_score = action, score

    # Logging
    print(f"Chosen action: {best_action}, score: {best_score:.2f}, evaluated {len(filtered_actions)} actions")
    return best_action
```

```python
from .adapters import copy_game, execute, make_value_fn, DEFAULT_WEIGHTS, ActionType
```

2) Replace decide() with this 1-ply implementation
- Paste the following function in place of the existing decide method:

```python
def decide(self, game, playable_actions):
    # Config
    max_actions = 20
    # Priority order: higher number = higher priority
    PRIORITY = {
        ActionType.BUILD_CITY: 100,
        ActionType.BUILD_SETTLEMENT: 90,
        ActionType.BUILD_ROAD: 80,
        ActionType.BUY_DEV_CARD: 70,
        ActionType.PLAY_DEV_CARD: 60,
        ActionType.TRADE: 50,
        ActionType.MOVE_ROBBER: 40,
        ActionType.END_TURN: 0
    }

    # Helper to get priority (default 10)
    def action_priority(a):
        return PRIORITY.get(a.action_type, 10)

    # 1) Filter END_TURN out if any build/trade/play exists
    has_build_like = any(a.action_type in {
        ActionType.BUILD_CITY,
        ActionType.BUILD_SETTLEMENT,
        ActionType.BUILD_ROAD,
        ActionType.BUY_DEV_CARD,
        ActionType.PLAY_DEV_CARD,
    } for a in playable_actions)

    filtered = []
    for a in playable_actions:
        if a.action_type == ActionType.END_TURN and has_build_like:
            continue
        filtered.append(a)

    if not filtered:
        # nothing but END_TURN -> return it
        return playable_actions[0]  # fallback

    # 2) Cap number of actions to evaluate
    if len(filtered) > max_actions:
        # Keep highest priority actions, then sample the rest deterministically by priority
        filtered = sorted(filtered, key=lambda x: action_priority(x), reverse=True)[:max_actions]

    # 3) Prepare value function
    try:
        value_fn = make_value_fn("base_fn", DEFAULT_WEIGHTS)
    except TypeError:
        # Some environments only require the name
        value_fn = make_value_fn("base_fn")

    best_action = None
    best_score = -float('inf')
    evaluated = 0

    # 4) Simulate each candidate and score resulting state
    for action in filtered:
        try:
            copied = copy_game(game)
            # execute may have different signatures in different forks; try common call
            try:
                execute(copied, action, validate=False)
            except TypeError:
                # fallback to execute(copied, action)
                execute(copied, action)
            # score from the perspective of this player
            score = value_fn(copied, self.color)
        except Exception as e:
            # If simulation fails for any action, skip it and log
            print(f"[foo_player] simulation error for action {action}: {e}")
            continue

        evaluated += 1
        # Use score, then priority as tie-breaker
        if score > best_score or (score == best_score and action_priority(action) > action_priority(best_action)):
            best_score = score
            best_action = action

    # 5) Logging for debugging
    if best_action is None:
        # As a last fallback return first playable action
        print("[foo_player] No simulated action succeeded; falling back to first playable action")
        return playable_actions[0]

    print(f"[foo_player] Chosen action: {best_action}, score: {best_score:.3f}, evaluated: {evaluated}")
    return best_action
```

Notes on the code:
- Keeps 1-ply only (no chance node expansion).
- Caps actions to max_actions=20 (configurable).
- Prioritizes builds and buy/play dev card actions.
- Uses make_value_fn("base_fn", DEFAULT_WEIGHTS) if available; otherwise falls back to make_value_fn("base_fn").
- Uses execute(copied, action, validate=False) but falls back to execute(copied, action) if signature differs.
- Logs chosen action and score for debugging — ensure logs go to game_output.txt.

3) Efficiency & safety
- Keep 1-ply; do not add chance_children or Monte Carlo in this change.
- Ensure copy_game(game) returns a deep copy; do not modify original game object.
- If you encounter heavy execution time, reduce max_actions to 10.

from .adapters import (
    Player, Color, Action, ActionType,
    copy_game, execute, make_value_fn, DEFAULT_WEIGHTS,
)


class FooPlayer(Player):
    """
    FooPlayer: an iterative improvement over the trivial "first-action" bot.

    Strategy implemented here (1-ply lookahead):
    - Filter out END_TURN when there are available build/play actions.
    - Cap number of actions evaluated (default 20) for performance.
    - For each candidate action: copy the game, execute the action on the copy,
      and score the resulting state with make_value_fn("base_fn").
    - Choose the action with the highest score; tie-break by a prioritized
      action ordering that prefers building and development actions.

    Notes / debugging:
    - We only use the adapter surface (copy_game, execute, make_value_fn).
    - Print statements are provided to help trace decision-making during
      experiments. Remove or tone down if logs become too noisy.
    """

    def __init__(self, name: str | None = None):
        super().__init__(Color.BLUE, name)

    def decide(self, game, playable_actions):
        """
        Decide which action to take from playable_actions.

        Args:
            game: full Game object (read-only for this method).
            playable_actions: iterable of Action objects available this turn.
        Returns:
            One Action chosen from playable_actions.
        """
        # Configuration: cap how many actions we simulate to bound runtime
        max_actions = 20

        # Priority mapping for tie-breaking. Higher -> more preferred.
        PRIORITY = {
            ActionType.BUILD_CITY: 100,
            ActionType.BUILD_SETTLEMENT: 90,
            ActionType.BUILD_ROAD: 80,
            ActionType.BUY_DEVELOPMENT_CARD: 70,
            ActionType.PLAY_DEVELOPMENT_CARD: 60,
            ActionType.TRADE: 50,
            ActionType.MOVE_ROBBER: 40,
            ActionType.END_TURN: 0,
        }

        def action_priority(a):
            # Guard if a is None
            if a is None:
                return -1
            return PRIORITY.get(getattr(a, 'action_type', None), 10)

        # Convert to list to allow multiple passes
        actions = list(playable_actions)
        if not actions:
            print('[foo_player] No playable actions available; returning None')
            return None

        # If there exist build-like actions, avoid selecting END_TURN immediately
        build_like_types = {
            ActionType.BUILD_CITY,
            ActionType.BUILD_SETTLEMENT,
            ActionType.BUILD_ROAD,
            ActionType.BUY_DEVELOPMENT_CARD,
            ActionType.PLAY_DEVELOPMENT_CARD,
        }
        has_build_like = any(getattr(a, 'action_type', None) in build_like_types for a in actions)

        # Filter out END_TURN if we can do something productive
        filtered = [a for a in actions if not (getattr(a, 'action_type', None) == ActionType.END_TURN and has_build_like)]

        # If filtering removed everything (rare), fall back to original actions
        if not filtered:
            filtered = actions

        # Cap the number of actions to evaluate. Keep highest priority actions first.
        if len(filtered) > max_actions:
            filtered = sorted(filtered, key=lambda x: action_priority(x), reverse=True)[:max_actions]

        # Build the value function. Some adapter variants accept weights, some not.
        try:
            value_fn = make_value_fn("base_fn", DEFAULT_WEIGHTS)
        except TypeError:
            # Fallback: older/newer signatures might only take the builder name
            value_fn = make_value_fn("base_fn")

        best_action = None
        best_score = -float('inf')
        evaluated = 0

        # Simulate each candidate (1-ply) and score the resulting state
        for action in filtered:
            try:
                gcopy = copy_game(game)
                # Try the faster validate=False first; if adapter/engine doesn't accept it, fallback.
                try:
                    execute(gcopy, action, validate=False)
                except TypeError:
                    execute(gcopy, action)

                # Score from our POV
                try:
                    score = value_fn(gcopy, self.color)
                except TypeError:
                    # Some value_fn implementations may only accept game and return global score;
                    # in that case we assume higher is better and proceed.
                    score = value_fn(gcopy)

            except Exception as e:
                # If a simulated action fails for any reason, skip it and keep going.
                print(f"[foo_player] simulation failed for action={action} error={e}")
                continue

            evaluated += 1
            # Tie-break by score then action priority
            if (score > best_score) or (score == best_score and action_priority(action) > action_priority(best_action)):
                best_score = score
                best_action = action

        # If no action survived simulation (very unlikely), return the first playable action
        if best_action is None:
            print('[foo_player] No simulated action succeeded; falling back to first playable action')
            print('Choosing First Action on Default')
            return actions[0]

        # Debug/logging to help with experiments
        print(f"[foo_player] Chosen action: {best_action}, score: {best_score:.3f}, evaluated: {evaluated}/{len(filtered)}")

        return best_action

      experiments. Remove or tone down if logs become too noisy.
    """

    def __init__(self, name: str | None = None):
        super().__init__(Color.BLUE, name)

    def decide(self, game, playable_actions):
        """
        Decide which action to take from playable_actions.

        Args:
            game: full Game object (read-only for this method).
            playable_actions: iterable of Action objects available this turn.
        Returns:
            One Action chosen from playable_actions.
        """
        # Configuration: cap how many actions we simulate to bound runtime
        max_actions = 20

        # Helper: try multiple common enum member name variants and return
        # the first matching ActionType. This avoids brittle AttributeError
        # when different forks name members differently.
        def resolve_action_type(*names):
            for n in names:
                if hasattr(ActionType, n):
                    return getattr(ActionType, n)
            return None

        # Resolve commonly-used action types robustly (fallback-safe)
        AT_BUILD_CITY = resolve_action_type('BUILD_CITY')
        AT_BUILD_SETTLEMENT = resolve_action_type('BUILD_SETTLEMENT', 'BUILD_SETTLES')
        AT_BUILD_ROAD = resolve_action_type('BUILD_ROAD', 'BUILD_ROADS')
        AT_BUY_DEV = resolve_action_type('BUY_DEVELOPMENT_CARD', 'BUY_DEV_CARD', 'BUY_DEVELOPMENT')
        AT_PLAY_DEV = resolve_action_type('PLAY_DEVELOPMENT_CARD', 'PLAY_DEV_CARD', 'PLAY_DEVELOPMENT')
        AT_TRADE = resolve_action_type('TRADE')
        AT_MOVE_ROBBER = resolve_action_type('MOVE_ROBBER')
        AT_END_TURN = resolve_action_type('END_TURN')

        # Priority mapping for tie-breaking. Higher -> more preferred.
        _priority_map = {
            AT_BUILD_CITY: 100,
            AT_BUILD_SETTLEMENT: 90,
            AT_BUILD_ROAD: 80,
            AT_BUY_DEV: 70,
            AT_PLAY_DEV: 60,
            AT_TRADE: 50,
            AT_MOVE_ROBBER: 40,
            AT_END_TURN: 0,
        }
        # Remove any None keys (in case some ActionType variants don't exist)
        PRIORITY = {k: v for k, v in _priority_map.items() if k is not None}

        def action_priority(a):
            # Guard if a is None
            if a is None:
                return -1
            return PRIORITY.get(getattr(a, 'action_type', None), 10)

        # Convert to list to allow multiple passes
        actions = list(playable_actions)
        if not actions:
            print('[foo_player] No playable actions available; returning None')
            return None

        # If there exist build-like actions, avoid selecting END_TURN immediately
        build_like_types = {t for t in (AT_BUILD_CITY, AT_BUILD_SETTLEMENT, AT_BUILD_ROAD, AT_BUY_DEV, AT_PLAY_DEV) if t is not None}
        has_build_like = any(getattr(a, 'action_type', None) in build_like_types for a in actions)

        # Filter out END_TURN if we can do something productive
        if AT_END_TURN is not None:
            filtered = [a for a in actions if not (getattr(a, 'action_type', None) == AT_END_TURN and has_build_like)]
        else:
            filtered = list(actions)

        # If filtering removed everything (rare), fall back to original actions
        if not filtered:
            filtered = actions

        # Cap the number of actions to evaluate. Keep highest priority actions first.
        if len(filtered) > max_actions:
            filtered = sorted(filtered, key=lambda x: action_priority(x), reverse=True)[:max_actions]

        # Build the value function. Some adapter variants accept weights, some not.
        try:
            value_fn = make_value_fn("base_fn", DEFAULT_WEIGHTS)
        except TypeError:
            # Fallback: older/newer signatures might only take the builder name
            value_fn = make_value_fn("base_fn")

        best_action = None
        best_score = -float('inf')
        evaluated = 0

        # Simulate each candidate (1-ply) and score the resulting state
        for action in filtered:
            try:
                gcopy = copy_game(game)
                # Try the faster validate=False first; if adapter/engine doesn't accept it, fallback.
                try:
                    execute(gcopy, action, validate=False)
                except TypeError:
                    execute(gcopy, action)

                # Score from our POV
                try:
                    score = value_fn(gcopy, self.color)
                except TypeError:
                    # Some value_fn implementations may only accept game and return global score;
                    # in that case we assume higher is better and proceed.
                    score = value_fn(gcopy)

            except Exception as e:
                # If a simulated action fails for any reason, skip it and keep going.
                print(f"[foo_player] simulation failed for action={action} error={e}")
                continue

            evaluated += 1
            # Tie-break by score then action priority
            if (score > best_score) or (score == best_score and action_priority(action) > action_priority(best_action)):
                best_score = score
                best_action = action

        # If no action survived simulation (very unlikely), return the first playable action
        if best_action is None:
            print('[foo_player] No simulated action succeeded; falling back to first playable action')
            return actions[0]

        # Debug/logging to help with experiments
        print(f"[foo_player] Chosen action: {best_action}, score: {best_score:.3f}, evaluated: {evaluated}/{len(filtered)}")

        return best_action

import os
from .adapters import (
    Player, Color, Action, ActionType,
    copy_game, execute, make_value_fn, DEFAULT_WEIGHTS,
)


class FooPlayer(Player):
    """
    FooPlayer: an iterative improvement over the trivial "first-action" bot.

    Strategy implemented here (1-ply lookahead):
    - Filter out END_TURN when there are available build/play actions.
    - Cap number of actions evaluated (default 20) for performance.
    - For each candidate action: copy the game, execute the action on the copy,
      and score the resulting state with make_value_fn("base_fn").
    - Choose the action with the highest score; tie-break by a prioritized
      action ordering that prefers building and development actions.

    - We only use the adapter surface (copy_game, execute, make_value_fn).

3. **Efficiency**: Adding chance handling must not significantly increase runtime. We need safeguards like caps on simulations and early aborts.

Proposed Strategy:
1. **Chance-Aware Evaluation**:
   - Use `chance_children` or `chance_children_for_action` to enumerate all possible outcomes (e.g., dice rolls, dev card draws) and compute expected value.
   - Fallback: If `chance_children` is unavailable, use Monte Carlo sampling (e.g., 10-20 rollouts) to estimate expected value.
2. **Robber/Resource Heuristics**:
   - For `MOVE_ROBBER`, score targets based on opponent resource frequency (e.g., steal from players with high pip counts or critical resources).
   - For road/city building, prioritize actions that maximize long-term resource production (e.g., ports, high-probability tiles).
3. **Efficiency Safeguards**:
   - Cap total simulations (e.g., 50 per action).
   - Early abort if time exceeds a threshold (e.g., 0.5s per action).
4. **Tie-Breaking**:
   - Maintain the existing priority order but refine it for chance-aware actions (e.g., prefer actions with lower variance in outcomes).

Implementation Plan:
- Provide a step-by-step algorithm for integrating chance evaluation into the existing 1-ply loop.
- Include code snippets for both `chance_children` and Monte Carlo fallback modes.
- Add a heuristic for robber targeting and road/city prioritization.
- Define logging and efficiency parameters.
- Outline 3 validation experiments to confirm improvement.

- Ensure defensive handling for API variations (e.g., `chance_children` vs `chance_children_for_action`).
- Provide clear parameters for sampling, caps, and logging.

#### **Chance Detection and Evaluation**
```python
def is_chance_action(action):
    return action.action_type in {ActionType.ROLL, ActionType.BUY_DEV_CARD, ActionType.MOVE_ROBBER}

def evaluate_chance_action(game, action, value_fn, samples=10):
    try:
        # Try exact expectation first
        chance_outcomes = chance_children_for_action(game, action)
        expected_score = sum(p * value_fn(g, game.current_color()) for g, p in chance_outcomes)
    except (AttributeError, TypeError):
        # Fallback to Monte Carlo
        expected_score = 0.0
        for _ in range(samples):
            gcopy = copy_game(game)
            execute(gcopy, action, validate=False)
            expected_score += value_fn(gcopy, game.current_color())
        expected_score /= samples
    return expected_score
```

#### **Robber Targeting Heuristic**
```python
def score_robber_target(game, target_color):
    # Score based on opponent resource frequency
    freqdeck = opponent_freqdeck(game, target_color)
    return sum(freqdeck.values())  # Simplistic: total resources

def evaluate_move_robber(game, action, value_fn):
    # Simulate robber move and score disruption
    gcopy = copy_game(game)
    execute(gcopy, action, validate=False)
    base_score = value_fn(gcopy, game.current_color())
    # Add heuristic for resource disruption
    target_color = action.value  # Assumes action.value is the target color
    disruption_score = score_robber_target(game, target_color)
    return base_score + 0.1 * disruption_score  # Weighted sum
```

```python
import time

def _resolve_chance_outcomes(gstate, action):
    """
    Try to get chance outcomes for gstate after action.
    Returns: list of (child_state, prob) OR list of child_state OR None
    """
    # Try chance_children_for_action(gstate, action)
    try:
        if 'chance_children_for_action' in globals() and callable(chance_children_for_action):
            outcomes = chance_children_for_action(gstate, action)
            return outcomes
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Try generic chance_children(gstate)
    try:
        if 'chance_children' in globals() and callable(chance_children):
            outcomes = chance_children(gstate)
            return outcomes
    except Exception:
        pass

    return None

def _expected_score_from_outcomes(outcomes, value_fn, color):
    """
    outcomes: either iterable of (state, prob) or iterable of state
    """
    if not outcomes:
        return None
    # detect (state, prob) pairs
    first = next(iter(outcomes), None)
    try:
        if isinstance(first, tuple) and len(first) == 2 and isinstance(first[1], (float, int)):
            # (state, prob) pairs
            total = 0.0
            for state, prob in outcomes:
                total += prob * value_fn(state, color)
            return total
    except Exception:
        pass
    # Otherwise assume list of states (equal weight)
    states = list(outcomes)
    if not states:
        return None
    s = 0.0
    for st in states:
        s += value_fn(st, color)
    return s / len(states)

def _expected_score_with_fallback(gcopy_after_action, action, value_fn, color, samples_mc=10):
    # Try exact chance enumeration
    outcomes = _resolve_chance_outcomes(gcopy_after_action, action)
    if outcomes is not None:
        try:
            exp = _expected_score_from_outcomes(outcomes, value_fn, color)
            if exp is not None:
                return exp
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Monte Carlo fallback: if we can get enumerated outcomes, sample them; otherwise return deterministic
    # If outcomes is a list of states, sample random choices from it
    try:
        import random
        if outcomes:
            # convert to list of states (expand (state,prob) -> weighted list)
            weighted = []
            first = outcomes[0]
            if isinstance(first, tuple) and len(first) == 2 and isinstance(first[1], (float, int)):
                for st, p in outcomes:
                    # push each state into list proportional to p (but cap to small multiplier)
                    count = max(1, int(round(p * 100)))
                    weighted.extend([st] * count)
                total = 0.0
                for _ in range(min(samples_mc, len(weighted))):
                    st = random.choice(weighted)
                    total += value_fn(st, color)
                return total / samples_mc
            else:
                # outcomes is list of states
                total = 0.0
                for _ in range(samples_mc):
                    st = random.choice(outcomes)
                    total += value_fn(st, color)
                return total / samples_mc
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Last fallback: deterministic score of the current copied state
    try:
        return value_fn(gcopy_after_action, color)
    except Exception:
        return -float('inf')

# Integrate into decide()
def decide(self, game, playable_actions):
    max_actions = 20
    samples_mc = 10
    max_simulations_per_action = 50
    PRIORITY = self._PRIORITY  # assuming you built PRIORITY earlier via resolver; otherwise rebuild here

    value_fn = None
    try:
        value_fn = make_value_fn("base_fn", DEFAULT_WEIGHTS)
    except TypeError:
        value_fn = make_value_fn("base_fn")

    # Filter END_TURN if build-like exists (existing logic)
    has_build_like = any(a.action_type in getattr(self, 'build_like_types', set()) for a in playable_actions)
    filtered = [a for a in playable_actions if not (a.action_type == ActionType.END_TURN and has_build_like)]
    if not filtered:
        return playable_actions[0]

    # Cap actions
    if len(filtered) > max_actions:
        filtered = sorted(filtered, key=lambda x: PRIORITY.get(x.action_type, 10), reverse=True)[:max_actions]

    best_action = None
    best_score = -float('inf')
    evaluated = 0
    sims_done = 0

    for action in filtered:
        if sims_done >= max_simulations_per_action:
            break
        start = time.time()
        try:
            gcopy = copy_game(game)
            try:
                execute(gcopy, action, validate=False)
            except TypeError:
                execute(gcopy, action)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[foo_player] simulation setup failed for action {action}: {e}")
            continue

        # Determine if action leads to chance outcomes (ROLL/BUY_DEV/MOVE_ROBBER etc.)
        is_chance = False
        try:
            if action.action_type in {ActionType.ROLL, getattr(ActionType, 'BUY_DEVELOPMENT_CARD', None), getattr(ActionType, 'BUY_DEV_CARD', None)}:
                is_chance = True
        except Exception:
            is_chance = False

        # Compute expected score
        if is_chance:
            score = _expected_score_with_fallback(gcopy, action, value_fn, self.color, samples_mc=samples_mc)
        else:
            try:
                score = value_fn(gcopy, self.color)
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"[foo_player] value_fn error for action {action}: {e}")
                continue

        # Robber heuristic tweak: slightly boost MOVE_ROBBER actions that target high-value opponents
        try:
            if action.action_type == getattr(ActionType, 'MOVE_ROBBER', None):
                # small heuristic: prefer moves that steal resources (if action has .value or .target)
                disruption = 0.0
                if hasattr(action, 'value') and action.value is not None:
                    # crude proxy: add a small bonus
                    disruption += 0.2
                score += 0.1 * disruption
        except Exception:
            pass

        sims_done += 1
        evaluated += 1
        # tie-break by priority
        pri = PRIORITY.get(action.action_type, 0)
        if score > best_score or (score == best_score and pri > PRIORITY.get(getattr(best_action, 'action_type', None), 0)):
            best_score = score
            best_action = action

        # time budget per action
        if time.time() - start > 0.5:
            print(f"[foo_player] time budget exceeded for action {action}, breaking early")
            break

    if best_action is None:
        return playable_actions[0]

    print(f"[foo_player] Chosen action: {best_action}, expected_score: {best_score:.3f}, evaluated: {evaluated}")
    return best_action
```

class FooPlayer(Player):
    """
    FooPlayer: an iterative improvement over the trivial "first-action" bot.

    Implemented strategy (1-ply lookahead + chance-awareness):
    - Filter out END_TURN when there are available build/play actions.
    - Cap number of actions evaluated (default 20) for performance.
    - For each candidate action:
      * copy the game, execute the action on the copy,
      * if the action produces stochastic outcomes use chance_children_for_action
        to compute an expected score (fallback to Monte Carlo / deterministic score),
      * otherwise score deterministically with make_value_fn("base_fn").
    - Choose the action with the highest expected score; tie-break by a prioritized
      action ordering that prefers building and development actions.

    Notes:
    - This class uses the adapters surface only. The code is defensive about
      varying adapter signatures and enum name variants across forks.
    - Print statements are included to help trace decision-making during
      experiments. Keep them for debugging; remove or lower verbosity when
      logs become too large.
    """

    def __init__(self, name: str | None = None):
        super().__init__(Color.BLUE, name)

    def decide(self, game, playable_actions):
        """
        Decide which action to take from playable_actions.

        Args:
            game: full Game object (read-only for this method).
            playable_actions: iterable of Action objects available this turn.
        Returns:
            One Action chosen from playable_actions.
        """
        # Configurable knobs
        max_actions = 20  # cap number of candidate actions to evaluate
        samples_mc = 10  # Monte Carlo fallback samples per chance action
        max_simulations = 200  # cap total simulations across all actions
        time_budget_per_action = 0.5  # seconds; abort expensive action sims past this

        # Helper: robustly resolve ActionType members across forks
        def resolve_action_type(*names):
            for n in names:
                if hasattr(ActionType, n):
                    return getattr(ActionType, n)
            return None

        # Common action types (fallback-safe)
        AT_BUILD_CITY = resolve_action_type('BUILD_CITY')
        AT_BUILD_SETTLEMENT = resolve_action_type('BUILD_SETTLEMENT', 'BUILD_SETTLES')
        AT_BUILD_ROAD = resolve_action_type('BUILD_ROAD', 'BUILD_ROADS')
        AT_BUY_DEV = resolve_action_type('BUY_DEVELOPMENT_CARD', 'BUY_DEV_CARD', 'BUY_DEVELOPMENT')
        AT_PLAY_DEV = resolve_action_type('PLAY_DEVELOPMENT_CARD', 'PLAY_DEV_CARD', 'PLAY_DEVELOPMENT')
        AT_TRADE = resolve_action_type('TRADE')
        AT_MOVE_ROBBER = resolve_action_type('MOVE_ROBBER', 'MOVE_ROBBER_TO_TILE')
        AT_ROLL = resolve_action_type('ROLL')
        AT_END_TURN = resolve_action_type('END_TURN')

        # Priority mapping for tie-breaking. Higher -> more preferred.
        _priority_map = {
            AT_BUILD_CITY: 100,
            AT_BUILD_SETTLEMENT: 90,
            AT_BUILD_ROAD: 80,
            AT_BUY_DEV: 70,
            AT_PLAY_DEV: 60,
            AT_TRADE: 50,
            AT_MOVE_ROBBER: 40,
            AT_END_TURN: 0,
        }
        PRIORITY = {k: v for k, v in _priority_map.items() if k is not None}

        def action_priority(a: Action | None) -> int:
            if a is None:
                return -1
            return PRIORITY.get(getattr(a, 'action_type', None), 10)

        # Convert to list for multiple passes
        actions = list(playable_actions)
        if not actions:
            print('[foo_player] No playable actions available; returning None')
            return None

        # Build-like detection: avoid END_TURN when we can build/play dev
        build_like_types = {t for t in (AT_BUILD_CITY, AT_BUILD_SETTLEMENT, AT_BUILD_ROAD, AT_BUY_DEV, AT_PLAY_DEV) if t is not None}
        has_build_like = any(getattr(a, 'action_type', None) in build_like_types for a in actions)

        # Filter END_TURN if a productive action exists
        if AT_END_TURN is not None:
            filtered = [a for a in actions if not (getattr(a, 'action_type', None) == AT_END_TURN and has_build_like)]
        else:
            filtered = list(actions)

        if not filtered:
            filtered = actions

        # Cap number of candidate actions to evaluate
        if len(filtered) > max_actions:
            filtered = sorted(filtered, key=lambda x: action_priority(x), reverse=True)[:max_actions]

        # Build the value function (defensive to differing signatures)
        try:
            value_fn = make_value_fn("base_fn", DEFAULT_WEIGHTS)
        except TypeError:
            value_fn = make_value_fn("base_fn")

        # Helper: compute expected score from a list of (state, prob) or list of states
        def _expected_score_from_outcomes(outcomes, color):
            """
            outcomes may be:
              - List[(Game, float)] where float is probability
              - List[Game]
            Returns expected scalar score (float).
            """
            if not outcomes:
                return None
            # Try to detect (state, prob) pairs
            try:
                first = outcomes[0]
            except Exception:
                return None
            # (state, prob) pairs
            if isinstance(first, tuple) and len(first) == 2 and isinstance(first[1], (float, int)):
                total = 0.0
                for st, p in outcomes:
                    try:
                        total += p * value_fn(st, self.color)
                    except TypeError:
                        total += p * value_fn(st)
                return total
            # Otherwise assume list of states -> average
            total = 0.0
            count = 0
            for st in outcomes:
                try:
                    total += value_fn(st, self.color)
                except TypeError:
                    total += value_fn(st)
                count += 1
            return total / max(1, count)

        # Helper: try to get chance outcomes via adapters, with fallbacks
        def _get_chance_outcomes(gstate, action):
            """Return outcomes or None if not available.
            Preferred: chance_children_for_action(game, action) -> [(game_copy, prob), ...]
            Fallback: chance_children(game, [action]) -> {action: [(game_copy, prob), ...]}
            """
            try:
                if callable(chance_children_for_action):
                    return chance_children_for_action(gstate, action)
            except Exception:
                # Continue to other fallback
                pass
            try:
                if callable(chance_children):
                    batch = chance_children(gstate, [action])
                    return batch.get(action, None)
            except Exception:
                pass
            return None

        best_action = None
        best_score = -float('inf')
        evaluated = 0
        simulations = 0

        # Iterate candidates
        for action in filtered:
            if simulations >= max_simulations:
                print(f"[foo_player] reached max_simulations={max_simulations}; stopping evaluation")
                break

            start_time = time.time()
            try:
                gcopy = copy_game(game)
                # execute on the copy; use validate=False when available for speed
                try:
                    execute(gcopy, action, validate=False)
                except TypeError:
                    execute(gcopy, action)
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"[foo_player] simulation setup failed for action={action} error={e}")
                continue

            # Try to obtain chance outcomes for this executed action/state
            outcomes = None
            try:
                outcomes = _get_chance_outcomes(gcopy, action)
            except Exception:
                outcomes = None

            score = None
            # If outcomes returned, compute expected score exactly
            if outcomes:
                try:
                    score = _expected_score_from_outcomes(outcomes, self.color)
                except Exception as e:
                    print(f"[foo_player] expected score computation failed: {e}")
                    score = None

            # If we couldn't compute an expectation, and outcomes is None but action type is known to be chancey,
            # attempt a small Monte Carlo by re-executing the action on fresh copies.
            if score is None:
                is_chancey = False
                try:
                    if getattr(action, 'action_type', None) in {AT_ROLL, AT_BUY_DEV, AT_MOVE_ROBBER}:
                        is_chancey = True
                except Exception:
                    is_chancey = False

                if is_chancey:
                    # Monte Carlo fallback
                    total = 0.0
                    samples = samples_mc
                    for i in range(samples):
                        try:
                            sims_gcopy = copy_game(game)
                            try:
                                execute(sims_gcopy, action, validate=False)
                            except TypeError:
                                execute(sims_gcopy, action)
                            # If executing the action triggered a chance resolution inside execute,
                            # the state now reflects one sampled outcome; score it.
                            try:
                                total += value_fn(sims_gcopy, self.color)
                            except TypeError:
                                total += value_fn(sims_gcopy)
                        except Exception:
                            # If an execution failed mid-MC, ignore and reduce sample count
                            samples -= 1
                            continue
                        simulations += 1
                        if simulations >= max_simulations:
                            break
                    if samples > 0:
                        score = total / samples
                    else:
                        score = -float('inf')
                else:
                    # Deterministic scoring of the post-action copied state
                    try:
                        score = value_fn(gcopy, self.color)
                    except TypeError:
                        score = value_fn(gcopy)

            # Robber heuristic: tiny bonus for robber moves that target rich opponents
            try:
                if getattr(action, 'action_type', None) == AT_MOVE_ROBBER:
                    # action.value is often the target color/position; try to detect target color
                    target = getattr(action, 'value', None)
                    if target is not None:
                        try:
                            freq = opponent_freqdeck(game, target)
                            # freq might be a dict-like; sum values as a crude resource count
                            disruption = 0
                            try:
                                disruption = sum(getattr(freq, 'values', lambda: [])() if hasattr(freq, 'values') else list(freq))
                            except Exception:
                                # freq might be an iterable
                                try:
                                    disruption = sum(freq)
                                except Exception:
                                    disruption = 0
                            # small weighted bonus
                            score = (score or 0.0) + 0.05 * disruption
                        except Exception:
                            pass
            except Exception:
                pass

            simulations += 1
            evaluated += 1

            # Tie-breaker: prefer higher score then higher priority
            pri = action_priority(action)
            if (score is not None and score > best_score) or (score == best_score and pri > action_priority(best_action)):
                best_score = score
                best_action = action

            # Time guard
            if (time.time() - start_time) > time_budget_per_action:
                print(f"[foo_player] time budget exceeded for action {action}; breaking early for this action")
                # don't abort entire loop; continue to next action
                continue

        if best_action is None:
            print('[foo_player] No simulated action succeeded; falling back to first playable action')
            return actions[0]

        print(f"[foo_player] Chosen action: {best_action}, expected_score: {best_score:.3f}, evaluated: {evaluated}/{len(filtered)}, simulations: {simulations}")
        return best_action

import time
from .adapters import (
    Player,
    Color,
    Action,
    ActionType,
    copy_game,
    execute,
    make_value_fn,
    DEFAULT_WEIGHTS,
    chance_children_for_action,
    chance_children,
    opponent_freqdeck,
)


class FooPlayer(Player):
    """
    FooPlayer: an iterative improvement over the trivial "first-action" bot.

    Implemented strategy (1-ply lookahead + chance-awareness):
    - Filter out END_TURN when there are available build/play actions.
    - Cap number of actions evaluated (default 20) for performance.
    - For each candidate action:
      * copy the game, execute the action on the copy,
      * if the action produces stochastic outcomes use chance_children_for_action
        to compute an expected score (fallback to Monte Carlo / deterministic score),
      * otherwise score deterministically with make_value_fn("base_fn").
    - Choose the action with the highest expected score; tie-break by a prioritized
      action ordering that prefers building and development actions.

    Notes:
    - This class uses the adapters surface only. The code is defensive about
      varying adapter signatures and enum name variants across forks.
    - Print statements are included to help trace decision-making during
      experiments. Keep them for debugging; remove or lower verbosity when
      logs become too large.
    """

    def __init__(self, name: str | None = None):
        super().__init__(Color.BLUE, name)

    def decide(self, game, playable_actions):
        """
        Decide which action to take from playable_actions.

        Args:
            game: full Game object (read-only for this method).
            playable_actions: iterable of Action objects available this turn.
        Returns:
            One Action chosen from playable_actions.
        """
        # Configurable knobs
        max_actions = 20  # cap number of candidate actions to evaluate
        samples_mc = 10  # Monte Carlo fallback samples per chance action
        max_simulations = 200  # cap total simulations across all actions
        time_budget_per_action = 0.5  # seconds; abort expensive action sims past this

        # Helper: robustly resolve ActionType members across forks
        def resolve_action_type(*names):
            for n in names:
                if hasattr(ActionType, n):
                    return getattr(ActionType, n)
            return None

        # Common action types (fallback-safe)
        AT_BUILD_CITY = resolve_action_type('BUILD_CITY')
        AT_BUILD_SETTLEMENT = resolve_action_type('BUILD_SETTLEMENT', 'BUILD_SETTLES')
        AT_BUILD_ROAD = resolve_action_type('BUILD_ROAD', 'BUILD_ROADS')
        AT_BUY_DEV = resolve_action_type('BUY_DEVELOPMENT_CARD', 'BUY_DEV_CARD', 'BUY_DEVELOPMENT')
        AT_PLAY_DEV = resolve_action_type('PLAY_DEVELOPMENT_CARD', 'PLAY_DEV_CARD', 'PLAY_DEVELOPMENT')
        AT_TRADE = resolve_action_type('TRADE')
        AT_MOVE_ROBBER = resolve_action_type('MOVE_ROBBER', 'MOVE_ROBBER_TO_TILE')
        AT_ROLL = resolve_action_type('ROLL')
        AT_END_TURN = resolve_action_type('END_TURN')

        # Priority mapping for tie-breaking. Higher -> more preferred.
        _priority_map = {
            AT_BUILD_CITY: 100,
            AT_BUILD_SETTLEMENT: 90,
            AT_BUILD_ROAD: 80,
            AT_BUY_DEV: 70,
            AT_PLAY_DEV: 60,
            AT_TRADE: 50,
            AT_MOVE_ROBBER: 40,
            AT_END_TURN: 0,
        }
        PRIORITY = {k: v for k, v in _priority_map.items() if k is not None}

        def action_priority(a: Action | None) -> int:
            if a is None:
                return -1
            return PRIORITY.get(getattr(a, 'action_type', None), 10)

        # Convert to list for multiple passes
        actions = list(playable_actions)
        if not actions:
            print('[foo_player] No playable actions available; returning None')
            return None

        # Build-like detection: avoid END_TURN when we can build/play dev
        build_like_types = {t for t in (AT_BUILD_CITY, AT_BUILD_SETTLEMENT, AT_BUILD_ROAD, AT_BUY_DEV, AT_PLAY_DEV) if t is not None}
        has_build_like = any(getattr(a, 'action_type', None) in build_like_types for a in actions)

        # Filter END_TURN if a productive action exists
        if AT_END_TURN is not None:
            filtered = [a for a in actions if not (getattr(a, 'action_type', None) == AT_END_TURN and has_build_like)]
        else:
            filtered = list(actions)

        if not filtered:
            filtered = actions

        # Cap number of candidate actions to evaluate
        if len(filtered) > max_actions:
            filtered = sorted(filtered, key=lambda x: action_priority(x), reverse=True)[:max_actions]

        # Build the value function (defensive to differing signatures)
        try:
            value_fn = make_value_fn("base_fn", DEFAULT_WEIGHTS)
        except TypeError:
            value_fn = make_value_fn("base_fn")

        # Helper: compute expected score from a list of (state, prob) or list of states
        def _expected_score_from_outcomes(outcomes, color):
            """
            outcomes may be:
              - List[(Game, float)] where float is probability
              - List[Game]
            Returns expected scalar score (float).
            """
            if not outcomes:
                return None
            # Try to detect (state, prob) pairs
            try:
                first = outcomes[0]
            except Exception:
                return None
            # (state, prob) pairs
            if isinstance(first, tuple) and len(first) == 2 and isinstance(first[1], (float, int)):
                total = 0.0
                for st, p in outcomes:
                    try:
                        total += p * value_fn(st, self.color)
                    except TypeError:
                        total += p * value_fn(st)
                return total
            # Otherwise assume list of states -> average
            total = 0.0
            count = 0
            for st in outcomes:
                try:
                    total += value_fn(st, self.color)
                except TypeError:
                    total += value_fn(st)
                count += 1
            return total / max(1, count)

        # Helper: try to get chance outcomes via adapters, with fallbacks
        def _get_chance_outcomes(gstate, action):
            """Return outcomes or None if not available.
            Preferred: chance_children_for_action(game, action) -> [(game_copy, prob), ...]
            Fallback: chance_children(game, [action]) -> {action: [(game_copy, prob), ...]}
            """
            try:
                if callable(chance_children_for_action):
                    return chance_children_for_action(gstate, action)
            except Exception:
                # Continue to other fallback
                pass
            try:
                if callable(chance_children):
                    batch = chance_children(gstate, [action])
                    return batch.get(action, None)
            except Exception:
                pass
            return None

        best_action = None
        best_score = -float('inf')
        evaluated = 0
        simulations = 0

        # Iterate candidates
        for action in filtered:
            if simulations >= max_simulations:
                print(f"[foo_player] reached max_simulations={max_simulations}; stopping evaluation")
                break

            start_time = time.time()
            try:
                gcopy = copy_game(game)
                # execute on the copy; use validate=False when available for speed
                try:
                    execute(gcopy, action, validate=False)
                except TypeError:
                    execute(gcopy, action)
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"[foo_player] simulation setup failed for action={action} error={e}")
                continue

            # Try to obtain chance outcomes for this executed action/state
            outcomes = None
            try:
                outcomes = _get_chance_outcomes(gcopy, action)
            except Exception:
                outcomes = None

            score = None
            # If outcomes returned, compute expected score exactly
            if outcomes:
                try:
                    score = _expected_score_from_outcomes(outcomes, self.color)
                except Exception as e:
                    print(f"[foo_player] expected score computation failed: {e}")
                    score = None

            # If we couldn't compute an expectation, and outcomes is None but action type is known to be chancey,
            # attempt a small Monte Carlo by re-executing the action on fresh copies.
            if score is None:
                is_chancey = False
                try:
                    if getattr(action, 'action_type', None) in {AT_ROLL, AT_BUY_DEV, AT_MOVE_ROBBER}:
                        is_chancey = True
                except Exception:
                    is_chancey = False

                if is_chancey:
                    # Monte Carlo fallback
                    total = 0.0
                    samples = samples_mc
                    for i in range(samples):
                        try:
                            sims_gcopy = copy_game(game)
                            try:
                                execute(sims_gcopy, action, validate=False)
                            except TypeError:
                                execute(sims_gcopy, action)
                            # If executing the action triggered a chance resolution inside execute,
                            # the state now reflects one sampled outcome; score it.
                            try:
                                total += value_fn(sims_gcopy, self.color)
                            except TypeError:
                                total += value_fn(sims_gcopy)
                        except Exception:
                            # If an execution failed mid-MC, ignore and reduce sample count
                            samples -= 1
                            continue
                        simulations += 1
                        if simulations >= max_simulations:
                            break
                    if samples > 0:
                        score = total / samples
                    else:
                        score = -float('inf')
                else:
                    # Deterministic scoring of the post-action copied state
                    try:
                        score = value_fn(gcopy, self.color)
                    except TypeError:
                        score = value_fn(gcopy)

            # Robber heuristic: tiny bonus for robber moves that target rich opponents
            try:
                if getattr(action, 'action_type', None) == AT_MOVE_ROBBER:
                    # action.value is often the target color/position; try to detect target color
                    target = getattr(action, 'value', None)
                    if target is not None:
                        try:
                            freq = opponent_freqdeck(game, target)
                            # freq might be a dict-like; sum values as a crude resource count
                            disruption = 0
                            try:
                                disruption = sum(getattr(freq, 'values', lambda: [])() if hasattr(freq, 'values') else list(freq))
                            except Exception:
                                # freq might be an iterable
                                try:
                                    disruption = sum(freq)
                                except Exception:
                                    disruption = 0
                            # small weighted bonus
                            score = (score or 0.0) + 0.05 * disruption
                        except Exception:
                            pass
            except Exception:
                pass

            simulations += 1
            evaluated += 1

            # Tie-breaker: prefer higher score then higher priority
            pri = action_priority(action)
            if (score is not None and score > best_score) or (score == best_score and pri > action_priority(best_action)):
                best_score = score
                best_action = action

            # Time guard
            if (time.time() - start_time) > time_budget_per_action:
                print(f"[foo_player] time budget exceeded for action {action}; breaking early for this action")
                # don't abort entire loop; continue to next action
                continue

        if best_action is None:
            print('[foo_player] No simulated action succeeded; falling back to first playable action')
            return actions[0]

        print(f"[foo_player] Chosen action: {best_action}, expected_score: {best_score:.3f}, evaluated: {evaluated}/{len(filtered)}, simulations: {simulations}")
        return best_action

```python
# Heuristic bonuses to nudge priorities (parameters above)
city_bonus = 3.0
settlement_bonus = 1.0
road_extension_bonus = 0.8
road_connects_settlement_bonus = 1.5
knight_play_bonus = 2.5

try:
    atype = action.action_type
    # BUILD_CITY: immediate VP increase + long-term production (small extra)
    if atype == getattr(ActionType, 'BUILD_CITY', None):
        # Optionally check this action upgrades an existing settlement (true city upgrade)
        # If so, give larger bonus
        score += city_bonus

    # BUILD_SETTLEMENT: favor high-pip or diversification
    elif atype == getattr(ActionType, 'BUILD_SETTLEMENT', None):
        node = getattr(action, 'value', None)
        if node is not None:
            sv = estimate_settlement_node_value(game, node, self.color)  # implement below
            # normalize sv to small range then add

    # BUILD_ROAD: prefer extensions and roads that connect to settlement spots or increase longest-road

        if edge is not None:

                # small bonus for keeping expansion alive

            score += knight_play_bonus

except Exception as e:
    # defensive: do not crash - log and continue
    print(f"[foo_player] heuristic error for action {action}: {e}")

# cap score adjustments (safety)
if score - base_score > max_bonus_scale:
    score = base_score + max_bonus_scale
```

```python
def estimate_settlement_node_value(game, node_id, color):
    # Simple proxy: sum pip probability of adjacent tiles that player does not already have access to
    total = 0.0
    # game.board.node_tiles(node_id) -> list of tile ids; tile.pip -> pip weight
    try:
        tiles = game.board.node_tiles(node_id)
        owned_types = player_resource_types(game, color)  # implement small helper enumerating resource types player has
        for t in tiles:
            pip = getattr(game.board, 'tile_pip_probability', lambda x: 1)(t)  # adapt
            # reward tiles that add new resource types or high pip
            # if tile.resource_type not in owned_types: bonus
            total += pip
    except Exception:
        total = 0.0
    return total  # raw sum; caller normalizes
```
- road_extends_toward_open_spot:
```python
def road_extends_toward_open_spot(game, edge_id, color):
    # crude: check if the road touches a node that is empty (no settlement) and is reachable
    try:
        nodes = game.board.edge_nodes(edge_id)
        for n in nodes:
            if game.board.node_owner(n) is None:
                return True
    except Exception:
        pass
    return False
```
- will_gain_largest_army:
```python
def will_gain_largest_army(game, color):
    try:
        my_knights = game.player_state(color).knights_played
        opp_knights = max(game.player_state(c).knights_played for c in game.other_players(color))
        # if playing one more knight puts us above opponents by >=2 (or just above)
        return (my_knights + 1) > opp_knights
    except Exception:
        return False
```

```python
def estimate_settlement_node_value(self, game, node_id, color):
    total = 0.0
    try:
        tiles = game.state.board.node_tiles(node_id)
        for tile in tiles:
            pip = game.state.board.tile_pip_probability(tile)
            total += pip
    except Exception:
        pass
    return total
```

#### **`road_extends_toward_open_spot`**
```python
def road_extends_toward_open_spot(self, game, edge_id, color):
    try:
        nodes = game.state.board.edge_nodes(edge_id)
        for node in nodes:
            if game.state.board.node_owner(node) is None:
                return True
    except Exception:
        pass
    return False
```

#### **`will_gain_largest_army`**
```python
def will_gain_largest_army(self, game, color):
    try:
        my_knights = game.state.players[color].knights_played
        opp_knights = max(p.knights_played for p in game.state.players.values() if p.color != color)
        return (my_knights + 1) > opp_knights
    except Exception:
        return False
```

Tool Calls:

from .adapters import (
    Player,
    Color,
    Action,
    ActionType,
    copy_game,
    execute,
    make_value_fn,
    DEFAULT_WEIGHTS,
    chance_children_for_action,
    chance_children,
    opponent_freqdeck,
